It's just been really hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's been going on for about a year and three months now. Um, yeah, it started right around the time our older relative moved in with us.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since you started feeling worn out?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad or depressed. I mean, I have days where I'm a little down, but it's not most of the time. It's really just the exhaustion that's getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep, such as difficulty falling asleep or staying asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. Um, most nights I get in bed feeling really tired, but then I just lie there. I end up going over the bills in my head, or my work schedule, or just thinking about whether everyone at home is okay. And even when I do finally fall asleep, I wake up feeling like I never really settled down.\n\nDoctor: Did the older relative who moved in with you have any significant health issues or pass away?\n\nPatient: No, he didn't pass away or anything like that. He just... well, he needed a place to stay, so he moved in with us. He's getting older, but he doesn't have any major health issues that I know of. It's just another person in the house, you know? Another thing to worry about.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble concentrating on your work or daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Like, at work... I can be standing at the register, looking right at the screen, and still just completely lose track of what I'm doing. My mind just wanders off to thinking about things at home or worrying about the bills. And because of that, I end up having to recount the drawer or catch a small mistake I almost made. It's just hard to stay focused on what's right in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It feels like my mind is always jumping from one thing to the next. I'll be worrying about money, and then I'm thinking about the relative staying with us, or how stressed my wife is. Then it's on to my church duties, or wondering if I'm messing up at work. It just doesn't stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying activities or hobbies that you used to like?\n\nPatient: I think so. I mean, I don't have a whole lot of free time for hobbies right now, just with work and everything going on at the house. But... yeah, when I do get a minute to watch a game or do something I like, I still enjoy it. I haven't really noticed any change there. It's mostly just that I don't have the time for it lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you get as much pleasure out of the activities you enjoy as you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Like I said, I just don't have much time for them right now. But when I do get to watch a game or something, it still feels the same. I don't think I've lost any pleasure in it, just the time to actually do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel irritable?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I'm definitely stressed out with everything on my plate right now, but I don't think I get angry or snap at people. I'm mostly just wrapped up in my own head with all the worrying, so... no, I wouldn't say I'm irritable.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I can be standing at the register, looking right at the screen, and still just completely lose track of what I'm doing. It's just hard to stay focused on what's right in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It just doesn't stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying activities or hobbies that you used to like?\n\nPatient: I think so. I mean, I don't have a whole lot of free time for hobbies right now, just with work and everything going on at the house. But... yeah, when I do get a minute to watch a game or do something I like, I still enjoy it. I haven't really noticed any change there. It's mostly just that I don't have the time for it lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you get as much pleasure out of the activities you enjoy as you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Like I said, I just don't have much time for them right now. But when I do get to watch a game or something, it still feels the same. I don't think I've lost any pleasure in it, just the time to actually do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel irritable?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I'm definitely stressed out with everything on my plate right now, but I don't think I get angry or snap at people. I'm mostly just wrapped up in my own head with all the worrying, so... no, I wouldn't say I'm irritable.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite over the past year?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My appetite has been pretty much the same. I eat about the same amount I always have.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, I stand a lot at work at the register, but when I finally get to sit down at home, I don't have any trouble just sitting still. So, no, I wouldn't say I get restless or fidgety.\n\nDoctor: Is the exhaustion you feel most days the worst symptom you're experiencing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. Honestly, that's exactly why I came in. I just feel worn out almost every single day. It's like, even after I get a full night's sleep, my body and my head are still just dragging when I have to get up for my cashier shift. So, yeah, the exhaustion is definitely the worst part.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel worthless or anything like that. I just go to work and do my job the best I can. And I don't really sit around feeling guilty either. It's really just the being tired all the time that's bothering me. So, no, that's not something I deal with.\n\nDoctor: Is the exhaustion you're feeling most days primarily physical, mental, or a combination of both?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to separate it, honestly. I guess it's just... both? Like, my body feels heavy and tired from standing at the register, but my brain just feels completely drained, too. It's just a general feeling of being totally worn out all over.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had anything like that. I don't feel depressed or down most of the day, even for a few days, let alone two weeks. It's really just the exhaustion and feeling drained that I'm dealing with.\n\nDoctor: Has your exhaustion and feeling of being worn out lasted for more than two years?\n\nPatient: No, not quite that long. It's been going on for about a year and three months, I'd say. It basically started right after our older relative moved in with us, and it's just been like this since then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often take naps during the day to try to feel more rested?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really take naps. I mean, I'm working at the store most of the day, so I can't exactly sleep there. And even when I'm home, I just kind of push through it. I don't really try to sleep during the day.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
